ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u10	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much, Mr President, for these questions, because I have an opportunity here to explain some of the things that have been medialy spilled. Firstly, the introduction of fees for each collection of water is a condition of the so-called. ex ante use of EU funds. This is a condition under the Water Framework Directive on the principle of reimbursement of the cost of water services. The European Commission has repeatedly ordered us to introduce, among others. fees for the collection of water for fish and other aquatic organisms, including for the purposes of, inter alia, cooling of thermal power plants, i.e. targets for which water has so far been free of charge. [[For mining, too?]] I'll answer later. I'm sorry. Now this is the case, that is, we have had a number of meetings with representatives of fish farmers, both carp and salmonids, we have had a number of meetings with most organisations - with most, not all - we have agreed on compromise rates and they amount per hectare of ponds from PLN 4 to PLN 6 per year. We agreed that for the collection of underground water for salmonid fish farming it would be between PLN 100 and 125 per quarter, and for the collection of surface water - also a similar rate for flow joints. How will this translate, you asked the Member for production, for fish farming and for the financial condition of these breeders? Well, according to our calculations, the cost per kilogram of fish increased by not more than 1,5 gr. 1.5 gr per kilogram of fish. So it's going to be a slight increase. I would like to say that we have really approached this with great caution, the fees are symbolic, they must be, but they are symbolic. Next question: What about Nature 2000? Of course, where Natura 2000 is and there are fish ponds, if fish farmers still want to manage there, they will manage. Let me add that the deduction of these fees will not result in such an increase in the cost of fish farming as to cause the closure of the farm, the elimination of these ponds. As I said before, these fees are really compromise. We've had, like, six meetings. There were protests, of course. Not all organisations, I also stress, have agreed, because some organisations do not want to pay any fees, and unfortunately we need to introduce this principle, the principle of reimbursement of the cost of water services. We are aware that fish ponds play a very important role in the ecosystem, that is without doubt, but it is also not quite the case that these water resources, which are collected in the ponds, are such a complete retention, because this is not a availability retention. In the spring, water is taken into the ponds, and in the autumn water is given to the river. There are losses related to evaporation, there are losses on sews. We don't go into the details of the evaporation losses, the losses on the sews, the other losses. Sometimes there are pollutions, too, unfortunately. That is why we introduced the principle of a lump sum. It's a flat-rate fee. If we started to analyze all the side effects carefully, it might turn out that this charge could be, should be much, much larger. According to economists' calculations, this should be at the level of tens of zlotys per hectare, if we put all this very precisely in this. There were further questions, these questions were in writing, among others. whether we take on responsibility and losses, the burdens caused by these joints. This is a public-law fee, it is public-law, and therefore there will be no compensation. There was also a question about what happened to the measuring devices. So far, we're not predicting measuring devices for carpy-like fish ponds, salmon-like ponds, yes. But I would like to make it clear that all measuring devices will be performed, mounted at the expense of the new Polish Waters organization. So that we will not burden users. However, we want some control on the actual amount of water taken. We need to use water rationally, we can't use water robbery. Therefore, we need to introduce, for example, some measurements that will allow us to check how this water is used. Thank you very much.

By law of 5 August last year, from 1 January, citizens have access to free legal assistance provided by a lawyer or legal adviser or, under their authority, an applicant. Where this assistance is provided by non-governmental organisations, tax advisers and graduates of higher law studies shall also provide advice with at least 3 years of experience in the exercise of legal knowledge-intensive activities directly related to the provision of legal assistance. Not all citizens will receive free legal assistance, but only those under the age of 26, natural persons to whom social assistance was granted in the previous year under the Social Assistance Act, persons aged 65, persons with a valid Large Family Card, combatants, veterans, threatened with, or injured by natural disasters, natural disasters or technical accidents. The thematic scope of such assistance shall also be narrow and include informing the holder of the applicable legal status, entitlement or obligations and identifying how to address its legal problem, as well as helping to draw up only a draft letter to the extent necessary to provide assistance. Taking into account the powers, subject matter and scope of cases in which citizens can receive free legal assistance under the Act, and comparing the subject matter with which they report to the parliamentary offices, the Ombudsman, the Senators' Offices and the Legal Assistance Intervention Office at the President of the Republic of Poland, the Act of 5 August last year meets a small part of citizens' needs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u28	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Mrs. Member! High Izbo! As far as the question raised by the Member is concerned, all these doubts raised here can indeed raise concerns about the scope of free legal assistance and the depth of this assistance. I'm sure it is. I know, because for many years I have been an employee of the Ombudsman's Office, which citizens expect help with, with which they turn to you, asking for help. With regard to these elements, which the honourable Member has pointed out, there is certainly a lot of reason for this. Indeed, first of all, the scope of recipients of this legal aid may seem too narrow - under the twenty-sixth, over the sixty-five... The question is whether, in fact, those people who, let's assume, are forty-six years old, not a big family, do not have a Large Family Card, have income within the range of PLN 800 and 900 per person, should not benefit from such help, and additionally have a specific problem, which is not just to obtain such information as can be obtained at the Registry of the court. I am going back to what the Member has said. Indeed, this scope of legal aid is limited to the preparation of a letter, true, and it is in the field of out-of-court legal aid. That is how it was formulated not to interfere with the assistance provided by agents appointed from office, whether in criminal proceedings or in civil proceedings. This, on the other hand, can undoubtedly give rise to doubts. There may be situations where those most in need of this assistance do not receive it, and it is addressed to those who either do not need it, or it is in such a form that you can go to the secretariat and ask whether the time limit for sitting is such or such. I can imagine. So I'm sure it needs analysis. Although the subject-matter of the persons to whom it is addressed does not include persons with disabilities. This was not in the question of the honourable Member here, but I am faced with these doubts. It should be obvious that this is a group of people who should be the preferred group in this area, right? Therefore, we analyse the operation of this law, collect data and are obliged to do so by the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. By letter dated 29 February, we asked the voivodships to report monthly to us, to provide information on the number of legal advices given, broken down by points held by lawyers or legal advisers and NGOs, and to provide quarterly aggregated data in this respect. These data will allow to observe the dynamics of this type of legal assistance and monthly processes. Therefore, we will keep you informed on this, and we will also be grateful for any kind of criticism and support for the voices of society. You have the best information and the best knowledge of what citizens need, what assistance they need and to whom they should be addressed. Therefore, every voice, every interpellation and offer of a meeting, even such a working, informal meeting with us, will be extremely valuable. We'll be very grateful for that. So that this question is also very important in this respect. To answer the question about the possibility of extension, I will say: there is a possibility. Constitution in this area, Art. 63 Constitutions which constitute complaints, applications and petitions absolutely do not prevent this, on the contrary, gives the possibility to change, to extend free legal assistance. I will remind you that at the time of the adoption of this law I did not participate in its adoption, but I know the period, the extent to which it was planned. Originally, there were two variants. The second option was assumed by the so-called. vouchers which would receive municipalities and persons with income below a certain threshold, we can estimate it at the level of PLN 800.1000 per head. Those persons would be entitled to such vouchers and with such vouchers a person could address the lawyer or counsel to whom he would like to go. It's up to her. This, of course, increases competition, but also reduces costs, because you do not need to maintain an expensive administrative apparatus, as it is today, in the form of a room in which an hour or two hours a day receives a particular person, specifically designated there. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u62	Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Speaker! High Izbo! The Head of the Office for Foreigners, because this question concerns the activities of the Head of the Office for Foreigners, makes an individual assessment of each application for international protection and no decision is issued automatically and the proceedings are certainly not template. This is also the case for Ukrainian citizens. Each applicant shall be given the opportunity to express his or her concerns about returning to his or her country of origin both during the hearing and at each stage of the proceedings in writing. His statements are then confronted with constantly changed, constantly updated studies on the situation in the country of origin. Very often these reports are produced by NGOs or international organisations, among others. those which you have been gracious to mention in your question, such as UNHCR or IOM or the State Department, Human Rights Watch and others. These studies are drawn up both by qualified staff of the Office for Foreigners, because there is an institutionalized cell in the office, which is dedicated to this type of development. Thus, each decision is based on reliable, exhaustive and individualised evidence. It is worth adding that the number of decisions by the Head of the Office for Foreigners repealed by the Council for Refugees and Administrative Courts is small because the Council repealed only 9,2% last year the decision of the Head of Office. In the case of Ukrainian citizens, this percentage is even lower as it amounted to 6.9% , and we are talking here about a very extensive procedure because Poland is one of the few countries of the European Union that have a two-instant system of administrative procedure, and yet a two-instant system of justice, so it is possible to verify this decision by three further instances. In my opinion, this confirms the high quality of work carried out by the staff of the Office for Foreigners. However, with regard to decisions refusing to grant international protection to Ukrainian citizens, it should be stressed that not all applicants come from areas occupied by separatists or engaged in war activities. People from war-free areas usually refer to the country's poor economic situation or fear of joining the army. These reasons have nothing to do with the grounds for granting international protection which the Geneva Convention establishes, as we know, which results in the adoption of decisions refusing them. In the case of applicants from Crimea and Donbass - I would like to stress this - the head of office every time, in every decision states that in these regions, these persons are threatened with persecution or serious harm, which implies the recognition that this person fulfils the conditions for international protection. However, the 1951 Convention, or so-called. The Geneva Convention on the Status of Refugees, the European Union Directive and the Law implementing it on the granting of protection to foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland require the authority to verify whether the person concerned has the opportunity to benefit from the so-called. alternative internal escape - the conflict in Ukraine is covered by 8% the territory of the country - whether the person has the possibility to move and reside in another part of the country. In assessing whether the situation in a part of the territory of origin complies with the above requirement, account shall be taken of the prevailing circumstances in that part of the territory and, that is to say, the personal circumstances of the foreigner. This means that in any case, the body is to consider not only the general possibility for the applicant to obtain protection in its own country, but also to examine whether it will be possible for this particular person to live a relatively normal life in a place where he or she could move to. This assessment takes into account the personal circumstances of the foreigner, such as sex, age, property status, family situation, health status, education, knowledge of languages. Otherwise, an internal displaced person, who is a young, healthy, well-educated and single man, or a single mother with four children, will be evaluated. If, after such an examination, the foreigner is deemed to have found refuge in another part of the country, the determining authority shall be obliged to issue a negative decision. In previous proceedings, the Head of the Office for Foreigners or the Refugee Council has already granted protection to Ukrainian citizens from Donbass or Crimea whose personal situation was so difficult that they could not be expected to receive proper care in the territory controlled by the Kiev government. These were among others. cancer patients, a family with a disabled child, a person suffering from severe mental illness or elderly people. Currently, the Head of the Office for Foreigners provides for subsequent decisions to grant international protection to Ukrainian citizens in a similar situation of life. The refusal to grant international protection does not close the applicant's possibility of finding refuge in Poland on the basis of other legal provisions, not only on the basis of narrow and detailed conditions of the Act on granting protection to foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The Polish system of granting protection is complete in this respect. Apart from refugee status and subsidiary protection, it also provides for the possibility of granting foreign nationals permission to stay for humanitarian reasons when a foreigner threatens to violate fundamental human rights in the country of origin or when returning to the country of origin would constitute an infringement of his or her right to family life or a violation of the rights of the child, which has often been forgotten, ending only with these two institutions. This applies to families... [[♪ The bell ♪]] Your Marshal, may I finish? There's really not much left.

From the meeting, support for Polish proposals grew, more and more countries joined this, to support our proposals, although there were still countries that contested Polish proposals and thought that the market should regulate everything. This was also the initial position of the European Commission. Within the framework of the Weimar Triangle, a common position was reached, which will be presented in a few days, on 27 and 28 June, during the meeting of the Council of Agriculture Ministers of the European Union. The Warsaw declaration, because it was named with the consent of all agricultural ministers, has 14 points and refers to the difficult situation on the markets of milk, pork, but also on the important market of fruit and vegetables for Poland. Ministers have proposed support in these markets from the European Union's financial resources, which should be particularly stressed - not from individual budgets, but from the Union budget - to support the mechanisms governing this market. Specific provisions on specific solutions have emerged in this agreement. PLN-1 million, while annually it pays the rent itself in the range of 1800 thousand PLN. zł. It can therefore be seen that the revenue from the tickets does not cover the expenditure of the museum to such a minimum. The Museum of Technology and Industry NOT applies annually for funding tasks in the field of science dissemination activities. Here I would like to remind you that, in accordance with the Law on the Financing of Science of 30 April 2010, such institutions may apply for task funding for science dissemination activities rather than subjective funding. Therefore, such a request must be examined individually each time. In 2015, the Minister of Science and Higher Education granted funding for the task of: The dissemination of Polish science and technology in 2015, but subject to the urgent need to develop a new solution to ensure the functioning and performance of the tasks covered by the proposal. What was the reason for refusing to grant the museum's funds this year? I would like to say that this situation has always been repeated in previous years - the team did not recommend the task because the applications submitted by the museum were not detailed, and only later did the ministry's decision somehow alter the recommendation of this team. In this situation, apart from others, the individual shortcomings of the proposal do not include the costs of organising all events in the form of a common cost estimate. The Minister for Science and Higher Education, in accordance with the applicable rules, may provide support to entities such as the museum only in the form of financing tasks which concern the dissemination, promotion and dissemination of the results of research and development activities. As I have pointed out, funding from science funds is tasked rather than subjective. Therefore, every time there must be a proposal and a detailed justification for it, which will justify the fact that the museum is financed. It must be said that, in the context of the financial difficulties faced by the Museum of Technology and Industry, there is no doubt that specific tasks need to be undertaken. Warsaw, Ministry of Development, Ministry of Culture and National Heritage, which will solve this problem and stabilise the functioning of the museum. The main problem is that the Museum of Technology and Industry is not legally a state museum. It acts as an organizational unit of FSNT NOT and has no legal personality. The role of owner is the Federation of Scientific and Technical Associations. Action on solving the difficult situation of the technical museum was also taken in previous years, in particular in 2014. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u104	Thank you very much, sir. I ask for your reply from the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of National Defence, Mr. Tomasz Szatkowski. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u105	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer the question in the context of current information, I have the honour to represent Mr Antoni Macierewicz, Minister of National Defence, who is taking part in an intergovernmental consultation in Berlin today with the Prime Minister. In the first point I will refer to the justification of Poland's participation in Operation Inherent Resolve and until this decision is taken. First of all, in the coming weeks, of course we all know this, the summit of the North Atlantic Pact will be held in Warsaw. At this summit, very important decisions will be taken in the spirit of allied solidarity. This will also include a very important groundbreaking reinforcement of the eastern flank. As a result of the decisions taken by the North Atlantic Pact, we expect to strengthen Poland's security by thousands of soldiers of allies with heavy combat equipment. This will be a crucial decision for the security of Poland. But the Alliance is an alliance of states that are located in... affected by various security threats, including threats from the south - a terrorist threat. We, as a country, are the object of some of the indirect effects of the crisis that we are facing in the Middle East. In this context, the expression of allied solidarity seems obvious. Operation Inherent Resolve is not an operation conducted directly by the North Atlantic Pact, but the North Atlantic Pact supports this operation in various indirect ways, and this will also be devoted to the talks at the summit in Warsaw. We are of course aware that the main Polish allies are involved in this operation and are counting on Poland's support. Moreover, when it comes to time, I will say this: Poland has been a participant in this coalition since 2015. In 2015, decisions were also made about some logistical support for the coalition, which will soon also be made public. It's about backup by transporting ammo. However, allies asked Poland to participate in a much wider dimension. These were requests for participation in exploratory support and training support. In this respect, certain decisions have been made in the Ministry of National Defence for a long time. These decisions were also accepted by Prime Minister Semoniak, they were not made. Moreover, in January 2015, Mr Grzegorz Schetyna declared the participation of the Polish Special Forces in NATO support activities in Washington. Given the image of Poland as a solid ally, given the very ambitious demands, the expectations of Poland to be realized at the summit in Warsaw, the implementation of these requests of allies comes at the last possible moment. At this point I will go to the presentation of details related to the participation of the Polish Army in Operation Inherent Resolve. It is an operation that is conducted within the framework of the Global Coalition to fight against the so-called. The Islamic State. The Global Coalition was established by the United States of America in September 2014 and responds to threats caused by so-called actions. The Islamic State. The legal basis is Resolutions No 1267 of 15 October 1999, No 2170 of 15 August 2014, No 2178 of 24 September 2014, No 2199 of 12 February 2015, No 2249 of 20 November 2015 and No 2253 of 17 December 2015. The UN Security Council has recognised that the Islamic State is a terrorist group and constitutes a threat to global peace and security. The goal of the Global Coalition is to finally defeat the Islamic State. The coalition operates in five areas: military operations, capacity building and training, stopping the influx of foreign fighters - terrorists, cutting down sources of terrorist financing, humanitarian aid and stabilisation support, fighting ideology so-called. The Islamic State. Currently, the coalition has 66 countries. Most of our allies are involved in various activities. Military aid is provided by e.g. countries such as the Czech Republic, Hungary, small Estonia or even Slovenia. In various other forms practically all our allies participate. With a view to solidarity with international efforts to combat terrorism, as well as the previous involvement of the Republic of Poland in the operation of the Global Coalition of a humanitarian and logistical nature, the President of the Republic of Poland signed on 17 June a proposal from the President of the Council of Ministers to use Polish military quotas in Operation Inherent Resolve. The basis is lex specialis from the Law on the Rules of Use or Residence of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland outside the borders of the State in relation to anti-terrorist activities. And that's what the article says. 3 (1) (a) 1 point 2 and Article. Point 2 (1) (a) c in conjunction with Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Act on the rules of use or residence of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland outside the borders of the State are the basis for such, and not another way of taking this decision. Following this decision, the Polish Armed Forces allocated two military quotas. The first of them, the Polish Military Quota of Operation Inherent Resolve Iraq, will carry out training and advisory tasks - will count up to 60 soldiers and military personnel separated from the Special Forces - with elements of the support of the quota in Jordan and Kuwait. The participation of the Polish Military Quota in Iraq will be an advisory and training character, as I said before, without involving Polish soldiers in combat activities, i.e. stricte kinetic, except as a result of the need for search and rescue missions. Special Forces will carry out their tasks on the basis of allied infrastructure in Baghdad. The main tasks of the Polish Military Quota will include advice and training of staff and special units of Iraqi forces. The second of the quotas, the Polish Military Quota of Operation Inherent Resolve in Kuwait, will carry out flight tasks in relation to imaging recognition for the purpose of supporting Operation Inherent Resolve against the Islamic State over the territory of Iraq in the composition of four F-16 aircraft with two DB-110 reconnaissance storage tanks. It will count to 150 soldiers and military personnel with support element in Qatar. In addition, PKW officers will be assigned to work at the Multinational Air Operations Centre in Al Udeid, Qatar, and their task will be to coordinate exploratory missions conducted by Polish aircraft. Separated forces shall not have a mandate to conduct impact operations beyond the situation arising from the right to self-defense in the event of an attack. The Polish military quotas will be used for the period from 20 June to 31 December 2016 in the Republic of Iraq and the State of Kuwait. They will be operationally subordinate to the Central Command of the United States of America, the US CENTCOM. In terms of national governance, they will be subject to the Minister of Defence through the operational commander of types of Armed Forces. The government administration body responsible for cooperation with the commanders of the operations will be the Minister of National Defence through the Head of the General Staff of the Polish Army. The Minister of National Defence will be responsible for the management and supply of the Polish Military Quota through the operational commander of types of Armed Forces. The Minister of National Defence through the operational commander of types of Armed Forces will also be responsible for moving the Polish Military Quota to and from the operation area and for insurance of soldiers and military personnel against the consequences of unfortunate accidents. The planned amount of expenditure for the use of Polish military quotas under the operation will amount to about PLN 52 million, including: PKW Kuwait approx. 30 million, PKW Iraq approx. 22 million. The project will be financed from the budget of the Ministry of National Defence. The detailed scope of the preparation, relocation and operation of the Polish military quotas in Kuwait and Iraq, with the division of tasks for the various organisational cells of the Ministry of Defence, was defined in the decisions of the Minister of National Defence of 31 May 2016. The task was carried out by the Secretary and Undersecretaries of State in the Ministry of National Defence, the Head of the General Staff, General Commander of Armed Forces types and operational commander of types of Armed Forces, the Head of the MON Security Officer for Flights, the Director of the Military Health Service Department, the Head of the Military Counterintelligence Service, the Head of Military Intelligence Service and the Chief of Field Officer. Schedule for the deployment of Polish military quotas in the area of the mission. As far as PKW Kuwait is concerned, on 20 June a C-130 transport group was relocated, i.e. 29 soldiers, to the Al Jaber Air Force base in Kuwait. On 5 July, the main component of the Air Force is scheduled to be relocated together with F-16 aircraft. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Polish Military Quota in Iraq - the relocation has already been completed. The current average for NATO countries is 3 The size of their armed forces. Poland currently participates in foreign operations at the level of 0.4% of the Armed Forces. The draft announcement of the year 2017 The Vistula River is supported by non-governmental organisations, which have been working for many years to improve the roadway of inland waterway transport in the Vistula River and activate local authorities from areas located on the Vistula River for cooperation. This project is rightly supported by the authority of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland today. The Civic Platform Club obviously supports this project, but let the establishment of the Year of the River Vistula draw attention to the fundamental problems of river management. The Vistula and its basin are one organism. There is currently a lack of coordination in the management of this organism. Water management is under the responsibility of independent regional water management departments. The flood shafts and some of the other regulatory works are responsible for the regional management of water equipment melioration under the Marshals of voivodships. IWT authorities are responsible for the surveillance of shipping. Several institutions are involved in the fishing industry. Regional Nature Conservation Directorates are responsible for nature conservation. The division of competences between Regional Environmental Directorates and Regional Water Management Boards is unclear and vague. The biggest and strongest user of Vistula waters is now energy, which does not matter to other river users and has no significant counterweight. Neither the Regional Water Management Boards nor the Regional Environmental Directorates have the courage to oppose the self-confidence of energyists, and negative decisions often result in sanctions against the Regional Environmental Directorates' Directors, whose attachment is too weak. Energy companies are used to the fact that water in Poland is free and without restrictions, they do not invest in any system of saving or closed cooling circuit. An example is the power plant in Połańc, which is expanding, but does not build a closed water circulation or any system that increases cooling efficiency, but only blocks the river with pneumatic jazz, taking all water to cool. Currently, shipping is closed in Pochań as early as 10 June and will probably not be opened until late autumn. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The power plant also dumps warm water that threatens the ichtyofauna in Vistula. Therefore, let this year 2017, when we celebrate the Year of the Vistula River, be also the year of administrative decisions that will help make the Vistula more accessible.

Józef's parents, writer Apollo Korzeniowski and Eve of Bobrowski, were independent activists sent to Siberia in 1861. Uncle Józef, Stefan Bobrowski, was one of the leaders of the January Uprising. Young Joseph left for Marseilles in 1874, where he took up the work of a sailor. Having problems as a Russian possessor, he moved to England and served there in the British merchant Navy. In 1886 he obtained the rank of captain and British citizenship. From now on he could, among others. command by cruises on its own. At that time, he began writing his first literary works. Finally, at the beginning of 1894, he completed the work of a Navy officer and settled permanently in England. In novels and stories written in English, such as: The madness of Almayera, Lord Jim, the core of darkness, Nostromo, the secret agent, or the shadow streak, included universal ethical reflections. His psycho-moral novels can be described as reflections on moral issues in the context of extreme living and working conditions of people - sailors during long and long ocean voyages or deep into continents. A study of Joseph Conrad's work is carried out, among others. international Joseph Conrad Society, as well as Polish scientists, among others. Zdzisław Najder. Currently at the request of Poland he deals with the great legacy and figure of Józef Korzeniowski UNESCO, too. Conrad is considered one of the most outstanding Polish and English writers. Filmed, among others. His novels and stories, such as Lord Jim, the duel, the kernel of darkness, in Poland it was the Blur of Shadow. In addition, the famous adaptation of the Kernel of Darkness is the time of the apocalypse. During World War I, especially the Polish-Bolshevik War, Józef Korzeniowski spoke about Polish matters. He called on Europe and the world to support the Republic against Soviet Russia. In 1920 he sought financial support from the United States for the Bolsheviks of his first and beloved homeland. The great writer and moralist died on August 3, 1924, in Bishopsbourne, England. I present the content of the proposed draft resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of 2017 with the Year of Joseph Conrad-Korzeniowski. On December 3, 2017, the anniversary of the birth of Józef Teodor Konrad Korzeniowski - Joseph Conrad - son of Polish patriots, exiles from Siberia, a great writer worthy of Polish and world culture. Conrad's writing achievements make him one of the world's most famous creators, who introduced values growing from Polish identity into world culture. It takes the undisputed position of the classic modern literature, which has changed the face of novel prose. He was a universal writer, taking on important topics for the Old Continent. More than 100 years ago, he outlined the vision of a Europe without borders as the basis for lasting peace between European peoples. The influence of Joseph Conrad's work on contemporary literature is timeless, and his works, among others. 'Lord Jim', 'The Blur of Shadow', 'The Kernel of Darkness' or 'The Secret Agent', have forever entered the canon of reading. The writing work of Joseph Conrad, which was created under the influence of Polish romantic literature, contains universal ethical values. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, convinced of the special importance of the writer's work and personality, establishes the year 2017 as the Year of Joseph Conrad-Korzeniowski. Both Korzeniowskis, mother and father were sentenced to exile to Volova in northern Russia. When the boy was eight years old, his mother died. My father was fired when he was already terminally ill. The orphan Konrad grew up in Lviv for many years. When he was 17, he was sent to France, where he started working as a sailor. He was an immigrant, not by choice, but by necessity. As a subject of the Russian and son of the convicts, he was obliged to serve for many years in the Tsar army. In 1878 he was denied an extension of his passport and could not serve in the French Navy. He moved to British. Then he began his career as a British sailor with a captain's diploma. It was an extraordinary career for the Pole. The fact that Korzeniowski's name is written in 13 different ways shows what problems he had with his origins. Even before leaving Poland and going to the sea, young Conrad-Korzeniowski announced that he would be a great writer. Years later, he claimed to have been an accident. In 1895, when the six-penny edition of his first novel "Almayer's Madness," he hesitated to issue it under his own name. He proposed his literary aunt's signature in the world, and he himself accepted the name Kamudi, which in Malay means "Stere." Conrad's writing work makes him one of the world's most famous creators associated with Polish culture. It takes the undisputed position of the classic modern literature, which has changed the face of novel prose. Addressing topics important to the old continent He is a universal writer who, in those days, outlined the vision of a Europe without borders as the basis for lasting peace between the peoples of Europe. After regaining independence, Józef Piłsudski co-created the Polish Army, became the Chief of State, the Chief of Chief and the First Marshal of Poland during the Polish-Bolshevik War, in which the victory protected the Republic and Europe from communism. In the following years II of the Republic of Poland twice served as President of the Council of Ministers, he was Minister of Military Affairs and General Inspector of the Armed Forces. During that time he had the greatest influence on Polish matters. Józef Piłsudski was the creator of the idea of close cooperation between the nations of Międzymorza, the creation of a group of countries in Środowo-Eastern Europe, which would constitute a barrier against the expansion of Soviet Russia and safeguard Poland's independence. During the period of German occupation and several dozen years of Soviet power over Poland, Marshal Józef Piłsudski became a symbol of the tireless struggle for independence and an example for subsequent generations of Polish patriots. In recognition of the great merits of Józef Piłsudski for Poland and in connection with the coming 100th anniversary of regaining independence of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, Józef Piłsudski established the year 2017 as the Year of Marshal Józef Piłsudski. The Committee on Culture and Media recommends that the High House adopt this draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The figure of Józef Piłsudski is still controversial today - some view him as a national hero, others as a dictator who has taken over power by force, illegally, by assassinating democracy. Named by the commandant soldiers, the marshal after defending Polish borders disappeared from political life. The young state began to create a democratic rule of law, the foundation of which was introduced on 17 March so-called. March Constitution. It was not easy to create a democratic state. After only five days of office, the final elected President Gabriel Narutowicz was murdered. For the years that followed, more offices were formed and fell. From 18 November 1918 to 12 May 1926, 17 changes took place as Prime Minister. The worsening economic situation, failed governments and a series of compromising scandals led to the next appearance of Józef Piłsudski on the political stage. On 12 and 15 May 1926 there was a so-called. May morning, initiated by the marshal. As a result of the coup, 379 Poles were killed. The era of parliamentary and cabinet governments in Poland has been completed. The coup led to the takeover of power by a group of Piłsudski supporters, called the Sanctuary, which, in proclaiming the slogans of moral renewal, filled many key positions in government administration. Piłsudski did not establish an open dictatorship, but he exercised authority in an authoritarian manner. Although he had no office, real power was in his hands. Depending on Józef Piłsudski, President Ignacy Mościcki and other Presidents of the Council of Ministers were both presidents. The political processes of the opponents of the new power were very frequent. The opposition was sent to prisons or to so-called prisons. places of isolation. As a result of the rule of Sanation, the freedom of the press or of the word and the violation of the independence of the courts was restricted. Although the May coup certainly was a crime against the Polish state, it is impossible to imagine the existence of the Second Polish Republic without Józef Piłsudski. History likes to repeat itself. There are many similarities between the present world and the world of our forefathers. Let us all remember that the answer to the incompetence of previous governments is not authoritarianism, [[♪ The bell ♪]] democracy, although it has its drawbacks, cannot be replaced by any radicalism. Marshal Piłsudski was a great Pole and it is truly not subject to discussion. But he was also a man, and like every man, he made mistakes. Let us learn from Józef Piłsudski's mistakes so that blood will never spill again in Poland. Ever Marshal Piłsudski...

Although Piłsudski's figure raises a lot of controversy, and the myths around him are sometimes difficult to distinguish from the truth, there is no doubt that he was one of the most important figures of the interwar 20th century. During the period of partitions - sibirak, one of the organizers of independence of the Polish Socialist Party, the creator of the PPS Fighting Organisation, the movement of the Polish Military Organization and the commandant of the I Brigade of Polish Legions, the leader of the armed struggle for freedom. After regaining independence - co-founder of the Polish Army, head of state, chief of Poland, the first Polish marshal during the victory in the Polish-Bolshevik war, which protected the Republic and Europe from communism. He was a man of charismatic and extremely strong character, but like every man, he also had his weaknesses. Perhaps not everyone knows that just before the Battle of Warsaw on 12 August 1920 Józef Piłsudski left Warsaw, went to the headquarters in Puławy and resigned from the function of chief of state and chief chieftain. In his letter to the Prime Minister, he pointed out that, since peace talks with the Bolsheviks gave nothing, Poland had to rely on the help of the countries of the hosts, and these made it dependent on the marshal's departure. However, Witos did not accept this resignation. In the following years the Second Republic was twice President of the Council of Ministers, Minister of Military Affairs and General Inspector of the Armed Forces. He was the creator of the idea of close cooperation between the nations of the Międzymorza, opting for the creation of a group of countries in Central and Eastern Europe, which would constitute a barrier to the expansion of Soviet Russia. Józef Piłsudski, especially in the context of the May upheaval and the policy pursued in 1926-1939 by the Sanitation Governments, is still controversial. As a result of the coup, Poland was on the brink of civil war. Its authors sentenced hundreds of innocent people - soldiers, residents of Warsaw to death. They had Poles shoot at Poles. They created a camp in Berez Kartuska for political opponents. Instead of serving the nation, they divided it into fighting camps. Ladies and gentlemen! There is no unblemished character and the figure of Piłsudski is undoubtedly re-colored [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the consciousness of Poles and idealized, Józef Piłsudski is one of the most important figures, which we owe to regaining independence in 1918. General Tadeusz Rozwadowski and General Władysław Sikorski defeated the Bolshevik hordes, stopping the march of the criminal system to the West and South of Europe. At the age of 18, he was associated with independent organizations, and at the age of 21, he was arrested by the Tsar police and sentenced to five years of exile to Siberia. In faraway Siberia, he met the long-term punishments of the insurgents of January, which only confirmed his conviction that he had to fight to regain Poland's independence. After his return, he was actively involved in independence activities. He formed the PPS Fighting Organization, conspiracy unions and shooting teams. After the outbreak of World War I on August 6, 1914, at the head of less than 150 losers, he set out from Kraków's Oleanders to fight for free Poland. This is how the legend of Piłsudski's legions was born. About Józef Piłsudski, whose 150th birthday anniversary we will celebrate next year, one can say that beside Ignacy Paderewski and Roman Dmowski he is the creator of Poland's regaining independence in 1918. May this motto guide us in constant efforts for the benefit of the independent Republic. In recognition of the greatness and importance of Józef Piłsudski in the history of Poland, the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle supports the initiative to establish 2017 with the Year of Marshal Józef Piłsudski. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u241	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media on the work on the parliamentary draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2017 by the Year of Adam Chmielowski - Saint Brother Albert. During the work of the committee saying these words, she presented the silhouette and the importance of St. Albert's brother, who helped to consolidate the most important social attitudes and gave Poles the hope of independence and social justice over the next decades. Adam Chmielowski was born on 20 August 1845 in Igołomi near Krakow. He was from a poor earthly family, where he was raised in the spirit of deep patriotism. During the January Uprising, when he was 18 years old, after losing the battle in which he was wounded, he entered Russian captivity, where his leg was amputated without anaesthetics. In order to avoid the repression of the Tsarian authorities, he left for Paris, where he studied painting. Everywhere he was, he was distinguished by his Christian attitude, and his strong personality had a strong influence on his surroundings. After announcing amnesty in 1874 he returned to the country and began searching for a new ideal of life, which was expressed by his painting. After a while, money from selling his paintings began to help the poorest, and his painting studio became a workhouse for the poor. With love for God and people, Adam Chmielowski resigned from his career and took over the administration of the heating room for the homeless. He moved there permanently, living among the poorest, helping them to bear not only material but also moral poverty. On 25 August 1887. Adam Chmielowski put on a gray Tercia habit and took the name Albert. He was a man of deep faith. He showed heroic love for his neighbor, sharing his fate with the poorest and wanting to restore their dignity. Despite his disability, he traveled a lot, founded new shelters, orphanages for children and young people, homes for the elderly and terminally ill. He died in the opinion of holiness, destroyed by severe illness and hardships of living in a workhouse. The funeral at Rakowicki Cemetery on 28 December 1916 became the first expression of worship universally rendered sacred to him. John Paul II, the Pope, now a saint, beatified him on June 22, 1983 in Błoniay Kraków, and canonized him on November 12, 1989 in the Vatican. He is the patron of the Albertine Orders and albertines, as well as artists in Poland. I trust that the celebration of the year of St. Albert's brother will be a great celebration of culture radiating all over the world. His life and creativity are synonymous with the cultural achievements of humanity, and direct influence and universal beauty speak to the audience regardless of geographical and cultural differences. The 100th anniversary of the death of Adam Chmielowski - Saint Brother Albert is held in 2016. In 2017, 130 years have passed since he put on his habit. His great talent and dedication to another man helped to consolidate among Poles the most important social attitudes and gave them the hope of independence and social justice for decades to come. Emphasising his outstanding merits in independence and in the field of social and artistic work, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, convinced of the special importance of his achievements for national heritage, he establishes 2017 as the Year of Adam Chmielowski - Saint Brother Alberta. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u243	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In 2016, it is the centenary of Adam Chmielowski's death, and in 2017 it will be 130 years since he put on his religious habit. Who was St. Brother Albert? The author of his life Maria Winowska in the introduction to her book lists the successive stages of his rich life: a soldier, an invalid, a painter, a monk, a beggar, a miracle worker, a man with God gathered. Adam Chmielowski was a hot patriot, insurgent from 1863, talented painter, forerunner of Polish impressionism, friend of Wyczółkowski, Chełmoński, Gierymski, Witkiewicz, Brandt, but above all he was a man of deep faith, a heroic servant for the poor and the handicapped. He was called the most beautiful man of the generation, the gray brother, the Polish poor woman of Assisi, the brother of all men. Charles Wojtyła in his play called him the brother of our God. In the service of the poor, Brother Albert emphasized the principle of universality, that is, taking care of all in the final need, regardless of nationality, religion or origin, because he saw Christ in everyone. The problem of material poverty and even more moral poverty, although in changed circumstances, is still present today. Moreover, in a materialized and laicized world we see a crisis of respect for human dignity, and this is one of the greatest problems of our time. Saint John Paul II during the audience for Poles on the day of Brother Albert's canonization, addressing the problem of rebuilding the common good in his homeland, recalled the principle of solidarity and social responsibility. He pointed out St. Albert's brother, as the patron of our difficult times, teaches by his example that the common good is not only a fruit of justice but also a selfless love for those in need. The Holy Father's canonization message to Poles ended with the wish that St. Brother Albert helped all Poles to regain mutual kindness and to become a living stone in building a civilization of love on our homeland and everywhere. And that is the message of St. Albert's brother for today. Today we need such attitudes, witnessing Christian mercy both individually and collectively. When the January Uprising broke out, he became involved in the struggle for his homeland's freedom. He was willing to give his young life for the independence of Poland, if such love was to be the will of God. After all, participation in defence of the country ended with permanent disability for Chmielowski. After finishing his painting studies in Paris and Milan, he decided to paint religious paintings. So he ascended into the law of the Jesuit fathers, in order to make fuller use of his talent for the glory of God. However, this decision was not successful for the artist. While in the novitiate, he was painfully depressed. It was only after leaving the congregation that his inner peace and spiritual and physical strength gradually returned. Painting the painting of the painting of the "Ecce Homo" became the beginning of a spiritual transformation for Chmielowski. By creating the image of Christ tormented, clothed with purple and ridiculed, he tried to convey the essence of God's merciful love for man. As he met poor people in the way, he saw more clearly in them the face of Jesus himself. Chmielowski realized that by means of art he was unable to give back what Christ had done for man. He decided to devote his life to the ministry to those who, for various reasons, found themselves on the margin of life in order to make at least some satisfaction for God. He became our God's brother. St. John Paul II said of him: "Holy Brother Albert did not write scholarly treaties... He simply showed how to do mercy. He showed that anyone who wants to truly do mercy must become a selfless gift to another human being. To serve his neighbor is to give himself first, to be good as bread. Adam Chmielowski's life is also a guide for all of us to be able to see others and give them real support. The Civic Platform Club supports the proposal to establish 2017 as the Year of Adam Chmielowski. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u249	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Adam Hilary Bernard Chmielowski, painter, critic and art theorist, was born on 20 August 1845 in Igołomi near Krakow in the land family. Very early he was orphaned and raised and educated by another family. He started his education in 1855 in the Cadet Corps in St. Petersburg. He continued it in the Warsaw Reality Secondary School of Jan Pankiewicz, and studied at the Institute of Polytechnic and Agricultural and Forestry in New Alexandria in Puławy. In 1863 he entered the armed war in the January Uprising. After the Battle of Grocchowiska, he was interned by the Austrians and deported to Olomouc, where he fled from, taking advantage of a favorable opportunity, entered the country and rejoined the regiment. Chmielenski. Wounded in the battle of Mełchow, lost his left leg. The family intervention saved him from being sent down to Russia. Convicted, however, of forced emigration, he left for Paris. In 1865, he returned to Warsaw, where he studied in the Rysunka class, probably under the direction of Rafał Hadziewicz and Ksawery Kaniewski. He continued his studies in Ghent, Paris, Munich. He was perceived at that time as an authority in the theory of ancient and contemporary art. In 1875 in Warsaw, together with Stanisław Witkiewicz, Antoni Piotrowski and Józef Chełmoński, he opened a painting studio at the European Hotel. The artists quickly found themselves in the circle of residents of Helena Modrzejewska's artistic and literary salon. Their atelier was visited by Henryk Sienkiewicz and Antoni Sygietyński, a composer and art critic, the leading promoter of the realistic trend. In 1880, he joined the Jesuit Order, but after six months he left the novitiate and went to Podole to his brother Stanisław, where he did apostolic work among the rural population. He maintained poor churches, roadside chapels, statues and altar paintings. He was prepared from the inside to the rejection of all temporal goods. For the rest of his life, he was guided by the maxim: neither home nor place nor anything. He traveled as a traveling painter, evangelizing among people, translating and encouraging. He aroused his suspicions of the Tsarian authorities, who found him dangerous by threatening to send him to Siberia. It forced Chmielowski to leave. In 1884 he returned to Cracow. In 1887 he entered the order of capuchin fathers and took the name of Brother Albert. Next year he became a member of the St. Vincent and started taking care of the homeless and poor cities of Cracow. He continued this work for the rest of his life along with his meetings of the Albertine brothers and sisters. He died in 1916 in [[♪ The bell ♪]] Kazimierz. Until the end, he was committed to working for the poorest. His funeral was a manifestation of appreciation and love. All of Krakow said goodbye to this most beautiful man of the generation. Along with the clergy, authorities, and the artistic world, a crowd of the poor followed the coffin. In 1938, the President of the Republic of Poland, Ignatius Mościcki, posthumously awarded Brother Albert the Great Empress of the Order of Polonia Restitut for outstanding merits in independence activities, in the field of social work, and on November 12, 1989. The Holy Father John Paul II canonized the blessed Brother Albert Adam Chmielowski in Rome. The Modern Club will, of course, support the resolution. Thank you.

After a while, money from selling his paintings began to help the poorest, and his painting studio became a hug for the poor. Adam Chmielowski also joined the Third Order as a secular man. Francis, and in 1888 he made vows and founded his own assembly of albertine brothers, and not much later - an assembly of albertine sisters. Over time, he devoted himself only to the poor, accusing almost entirely of painting. The outstanding Cracow philosopher Fr. He fought in the January uprising and in this uprising the bullet destroyed his leg. From now on, he was crippled and wore a prosthetic. To me, he was a strange character. I was very spiritually involved with him. I wrote a drama about him, which I titled the Brother of our God. I was fascinated by his personality. Tadeusz Kościuszko is a symbol of freedom and integrity not only for our nation. The purest son of freedom he called Kościuszka Thomas Jefferson, one of the founding fathers of the United States, paying tribute to his merits to the Americans. Kościuszko was the son of the Brest swordsman, an army officer of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania. He graduated from the Cadet Corps, where he also received an engineering education, which later facilitated his military career in America. Interestingly, his first dream was a painting career. He even went to Paris. He did not become an artist, but his stay in pre-revolutionary France was important for his political and social beliefs. After the first partition of the Republic of Poland, Kościuszko returned to the country to join the army. However, the army had already been cut off, so he went abroad again. Right after the declaration of independence of North America went there to fight for America's freedom. As a colonel of engineering, he marked himself in the construction of the fortifications of Philadelphia, Saratoga and the famous West Point fortress. The U.S. government has promoted him to brigade general in recognition of merit. After eight years he returned to the country and fought in the war against Russia, for which he received Virtuti Militari - a new award and the Order of the White Eagle, while the National Assembly of France gave him the honorary title of a French citizen for his merits in the fight for freedom. After the second partition of Poland, Kościuszko came to Dresden, where he and Ignacy Potocki and Hugon Kołłątaj developed the concept of national uprising. Kościuszko was a natural candidate for his leadership because he combined talents and experience from the war in America, and he had military trust and a strong international position. In 1794 an uprising broke out. Tadeusz Kościuszko became his warden. They were hailed as a bone-scene insurrection. It was the last attempt to save the Republic. The uprising covered almost the entire country. The greatest successes of this breakup are the victorious war near Racławice, near Szczekociany, as well as the Połaniec University which increases the freedom of serfs and the union in the fight of Poles of all states and religions. In the insurrection of Kościuszko Poles [[♪ The bell ♪]] They have shown themselves to be valiant and devoted to their homeland. Within 8 months, the Polish side mobilized 150 thousand. Humans, however, the armies of the two partitions bloody suppressed the uprising. The Battle of Maciejowice ended it on October 10, 1794, on November 4, the Russians entered Warsaw and slaughtered the population of Prague - 20 thousand. The victims, and they sent 20,000 to Siberia. Fighting. Despite the failure to rise up, the idea of fighting for freedom through the efforts of the whole nation has become a message to the posterity. The Poles took it up, chasing at the fight for independence of their homeland in every generation.

On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I would like to recommend to the High House the adoption of a project in prints No 345 and 508, i.e. the establishment of 2017 by Tadeusz Kościuszko. Tadeusz Kościuszko occupies a special place in the history of Poland. It is a symbol of freedom for us, a model of a patriot, an example of understanding tolerance and equality. Andrzej Tadeusz Bonaventura Kościuszko, born in Polesia, a student of the Piarist College in Lubieszów, chose a military career and joined the Knight's School Cadet Corps in 1765. He was an outstanding listener, taking an engineering course. Then he continued his education at the Royal Guard Military Academy in Versailles. His stay in France, on the eve of the revolution, had a huge impact on his political and social beliefs. After the first partition he returned to Poland, but because he did not find his place in the reduced Polish army, he went to America. His engineering skills were used in fortifications, among others. In the battle of Saratoga. George Washington entrusted him with the construction of the West Point fortress at the Hudson River. In recognition of the merit of the resolution of the American Congress, he was promoted to general of the U.S. Army brigade. He received 250 hectares of land and financial gratification. All his possessions there were devoted to the redemption of freedom and education of the redeemed slaves. After returning to the country, one of his first decisions was to limit the peasants from their family estate in Siechnowice and completely exempt women from it. During this time, a group of activists who saw the need for reforms in Poland played an increasingly important role in Poland. The four-year-old Sejm took up the work of repairing the Republic of Poland. The army was increased to 100,000, and in 1789, the king nominated Tadeusz Kościuszka as the rank of Major General of the Crown Forces. Poles' efforts to strengthen their homeland aroused concerns about Russia and Prussia and parts of the native magnatery related to foreign powers. The market confederation was established and the Russian army crossed the borders of the Republic of Poland. Kościuszko actively participated in preparations for the expected intervention. After the king's appointment as chief of the Crown Army, Józef Poniatowski Kościuszko became commander of one of the three divisions. In the Polish-Russian war in 1792, he was awarded the Order of the White Eagle in the Battle of Greens and Dubienka. After the second partition, Kościuszko developed the concept of organizing the national uprising and, despite many adversities, took a solemn oath to the nation and formally took the leadership of the insurrection. And even though the uprising ended in defeat, within eight months... [[♪ The bell ♪]] Mr. Marshal, just a moment longer, okay? ...mobilized to fight 150,000. People. It was the first break against the assassins. During that period, a legal act was issued called the Polesian Universe, which formally granted limited personal freedom to the serfs and enabled them to fight armed forces in the insurgent army. Kościuszko died in exile. Kościuszko, as a character who takes a special place in the pantheon of national heroes, symbol of freedom and patriotic design, not only for Poles, deserves to be declared the Sejm of the Republic of Poland 2017 as the Year of Tadeusz Kościuszko. Thank you very much.

The highest peak of Australia won by Paweł Edmund Strzelecki is Mount Kosciuszko - Mount Kosciuszko. General Tadeusz Kościuszko participated in the American War for Independence and in the Polish national liberation uprisings. In 2017, the 200th anniversary of Tadeusz Kościuszko's death. Tadeusz Kościuszko, precisely Andrzej Tadeusz Bonaventura Kościuszko's coat of arms Roch III, was born on 4 February 1746 in Merechowszczyzna, died on 15 October 1817 in Solura - Polish and American general, fortifier engineer, participated in the war for independence of the United States, raised up an uprising against Russia and Prussia in the Republic of Poland, the Supreme Chief of the National Armed Forces in the insurrection of 1794. For the first time, he demonstrated his unique military talent shortly after his arrival in America on 24 September 1776. He was assigned there to develop a fragment of the fortification of the city of Philadelphia, a region of the Delaware River. In recognition of merit, the resolution of the Congress of Tadeusz Kościuszko was promoted on 13 October 1783 to general of the U.S. Army brigade. He also received a special thank-you, a landslide, about 250 ha, and a substantial sum of money to be paid at a later date, in annual instalments. When Congress paid him back in 1798, despite a difficult financial situation, he immediately spent this money on buying the freedom and training of Negroes. All the rest of his American estate Kościuszko entrusted to Jefferson, who was the executor of his will with a clear abolitionist expression. A few years later, on 24 March 1794 he made a solemn oath to the nation and formally took the leadership of the insurrection later called the Kościuszko Uprising as the Supreme Warden of the National Armed Forces. Wounded at Maciejowice, Tadeusz Kościuszko entered Russian captivity. He was released by the Tsar Paul I in 1796. The general took an oath not to fight against Russia. And the Tsar promised to release about 20,000. Poles imprisoned after the defeat of the uprising. The Tsar has not fulfilled that promise. In 1799. Kościuszko met with Napoleon, who persuaded him to support the idea of creating Polish troops in France. Kościuszko rejected Napoleon's proposal and took a stand against the pronapoleon sympathy of most Polish soldiers. Between 1798 and 1815 he lived in Brevile near Paris with Piotr Zeltner and took part in the creation of the Polish Legions. In 1799 - October 17 and November 6 - he met Napoleon. Kościuszko was an opponent of binding the Polish case with Napoleon, whom he did not trust. In our consciousness he was the first in history warden of the uprising to raid Poles into armed action. He is a national hero of Poland and the United States. Tadeusz Kościuszko treated his military and civic duties as a service for the welfare of the whole nation. He deeply believed in the inalienable dignity of the man whose good he put first. Today, many monuments, streets, schools and other institutions of his name remind us of his merit and importance for the Polish state. Tadeusz Kościuszko died in 1817 in Solura, Switzerland. His heart rested in 1927 at the Royal Castle in Warsaw.

Reducing bureaucratic obligations - some of the solutions are due to consultation with entrepreneurs or the collection of their experience - to increase the participation of SMEs, but also to eliminate solutions that are detrimental to entrepreneurs today, i.e. above all, the question of using references and the problem of so-called. companies of folders, which use the potential of third parties, win in tenders, while SMEs are essentially imported into the role of subcontractors, which is necessarily beneficial in this whole contract chain, because the biggest advantage, the largest margin is always at the beginning. At this point in time, the contracting authority will be able to request that all the key elements of the contract be executed by the contractor, so that the process is very limited, especially in the construction market, this is severe for companies. Finally, the mandatory labour law clause, Article. 22 Labour Code, i.e. increasing the rigour associated with employment for employment contracts. Where the relationship between the persons performing the contract and the contractor will be governed by the Article. 22 Labour Code, the contracting authority will not only be able to, but will be required in the specification to require tenderers to apply labour law rules. Second question, about trading in income. This is, above all, an amendment adapting to the Directive. The Directive and the Regulation, which introduce a single European procurement document, use the concept of turnover. We did indeed withdraw from this amendment yesterday, when the arguments were used that turnover is not a legal concept but only an economic concept, and after the committee meeting we made an additional analysis. The concept of trading occurs in Polish laws, whether in the Law on freedom of business or in tax laws, but also in today's regulation on documents which contractors submit in proceedings. So it's not like I'm gonna say change... On the other hand, this present network of concepts is maintained, otherwise, given that the law would use the concept of income, and in a single European document whose application will be mandatory will be the concept of trading, the contracting authority will de facto not even be able to verify the conditions to be set up. Therefore, in order for a correlation to exist between the provisions of the Act, the Senate amendment and the revenue should have been supported to replace turnover. At the same time, as I say, we have checked it in other our regulations - there the concept of trading is as much as possible. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u297	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the adoption of the Senate on the Act amending the Law on Research Institutes, print No. 624. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. 54 (1) (a) 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed on 14 June 2016 the Senate's resolution on the above matter in order to examine it to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth. The Senate amendment concerns use in Article. Paragraph 24 5a of the word "decision' in the absence of a director of a state research institute. At that time, the Scientific Council shall manage the running of the competition again and, pending the appointment of a new Director, the Supervisory Minister shall appoint one of his alternates or another staff member of the Institute to act as Director, who shall fulfil the conditions laid down in the Act. The term "decision' used in the provision is characteristic of administrative proceedings. The Senate therefore considered that the terminology appropriate to the minister's activities should be used. Committee on Education, Science and Youth at its meeting on 21 June this year. it has examined the above resolution and asks the High Sejm to accept the Senate amendment. Thank you very much.

They shall perform a particular role in carrying out activities contracted by the competent minister. The report of the Supreme Audit Chamber of October 2015 revealed the inefficient exploitation of the scientific potential of institute staff and the focus of activities on the provision of non-research and development services. The activities of the Institutes audited largely consisted in the implementation of routine work for public administration and economic operators with little implementation activity - although the number of patented inventions increased twice, only 20% of them were applied in practice. Also, the previous supervision of institutes was not properly carried out - it mainly concerned financial and organisational issues, and to a lesser extent scientific and implementation activities. The Supreme Audit Board addressed requests for inspections to the supervisors taking into account the effects of their scientific and development activities. As you can see, action was needed to remedy the situation in research institutes and improve their functioning. This will be done by this law on strengthening the supervision of state research institutes by the competent minister. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u381	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Print No 642, Act amending the Law on Government Departments and some other laws. Ladies and gentlemen, this law, I would call it a cleaning law, refers directly to the statutory changes in the administrative departments of a few months ago. At that time new ministries were created and this law is a consequence of those changes, and I want to make this very clear. No new institutions are created, no new departments are created, no increase in employment in the state administration, no change in the powers of the state administration. The aim is to clear some competences between the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction and the Ministry of Development and between the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration and the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction. In particular, as regards the former Ministry of Infrastructure and Development, two ministries were created: the Ministry of Development and the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction. And that's where the transfer of powers comes in. As far as MSWiA is concerned, geodesia and cartography are excluded and incorporated into the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction. There is also an adaptation of the nomenclature associated with the relevant ministries. The explanatory memorandum sets out in detail all departments that are transferred between these ministries. In view of the existing competences of the Minister responsible for construction, planning and land-use and housing, the project envisages two changes in the transfer of powers to other ministers in view of their substantive competence. The first of these amendments shall be provided for in Article. 2 point 2 of the draft amendment of the Law of 31 January 1959 on cemeteries and the hiding of the dead. It involves amending the competent authority to issue a regulation setting out the requirements for cemeteries, graves and other burial sites. Thus, with regard to the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction and the Ministry of Development, these competences are shared among these ministers from the former Ministry of Infrastructure and Development. Ladies and gentlemen, opinions have been attached to this printing. As regards matters relating to European Union law, the draft law amending the Law on the departments of government and certain other laws remains outside the scope of European Union law. The other opinions of the Sejm Analysis Office are also positive and these changes do not conflict. Please consider this draft law and refer it to the relevant Sejm committees. Thank you very much. Only the ombudsman and the ombudsman have constitutional authority. The institution of the ombudsman, hereinafter referred to as the RPP, is a concept based on the experience and reflections of the first social ombudsman of the Polish Confederation of Private Employers Lewiatan. Such a public spokesperson was active in 2003 and 2004. The task of this social Ombudsman was to defend the interests of entrepreneurs in conflict situations to which the State is party, the institutions of the State. Disputes between entrepreneurs, employers and employees were not within the remit of the ombudsman. The ombudsman would be important and important for small and medium-sized enterprises, since the Ombudsman's interest is mainly in systemic matters, large, requiring systemic solutions, while small entrepreneurs, especially those who start business, are deprived of professional legal services. For them these are very often difficult cases, very often such companies, such enterprises have no care and fall, are outside the interest of state institutions. The appointment of an ombudsman would also guarantee individual entrepreneurs, this small, real exercise of their rights. Today, a group of small and medium-sized enterprises employs about 6.5 million workers. This shows how important this social group is when it comes to creating our national income, creating jobs, and therefore requires special support. As far as the exercise of its rights is concerned, as I said, the Ombudsman is not always able because of a large group of heavier cases, if one can so determine whether these systems deal with small business cases. Because he would take matters on his own initiative and lead them to a decision or to a decision by certain institutions or bodies. Forms of decisions could be very different. The institution would have 30 days to take the case. How would an entrepreneur's lawyer be empowered in a way? He would be appointed by the Sejm with the consent of the Senate at the request of the Marshal of the Sejm or a group of 35 Members. The procedure for submitting candidates to the Ombudsman would be laid down by a resolution of the Sejm on the appointment of an ombudsman. The term of office would last five years from the date of the oath. The same person could not serve for two consecutive terms. The earlier termination of the term of office may result only from the resignation of the Ombudsman from his post, his death or his dismissal. The Ombudsman in his business would be independent of other state bodies, which is important, and would only be accountable to the Sejm. In order to secure this independence, he would be protected by formal immunity. Impacts on the state budget and for local governments. We propose that the Ombudsman should be financed by part of the funds paid by the entrepreneurs to the Labour Fund. Entrepreneurs very often point out that not always the funds they pay to the Labour Fund are properly used by them or are not used to carry out their tasks. In fact, this would be a compensation that would enable businesses to operate better and have practically zero implications for the state budget, as this would be included in the measures that entrepreneurs themselves pay for the Labour Fund. Thus, for local government budgets, the financial consequences would also be zero. Our project is compatible with European Union law and does not fall under the competence of EU law.
