ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u434	Thank you. And please take the floor of Minister Jadwiga Emilewicz.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u435	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I will try to answer the questions and refer to the bill without a tablet and without a card. [[Applause]] At the same time, I would like to say that because we received the bill a few days ago, so what I am going to present here will not be the position of the government, as the recommendations for such a position have only just been sent to the government. It is therefore up to the Marshal's decision whether he wishes to hear the recommendations we have made to the government or whether he will acknowledge that our position, the government's position, is not yet ready to present them to you. Yes, Mr. Marshal. In the requests and recommendations submitted by the Ministry of Development concerning the draft Act on the appointment of an ombudsman, we submit five basic reservations and two general comments. First general remark. The explanatory memorandum of the Act states that the appointment of the Ombudsman is related to the possibility of realising its rights. Such justification would clearly indicate the surrender of the legal system. If we want to set up an authority to prove that all other regulations and acts in that country do not apply, then we do indeed admit to surrender. That's the first general remark. And there are five substantive objections. Some of them are already replicating the comments that Members have made here before me. The first comment we make is that we stand in the position that the government's task is non-nocere, not teasing, not disturbing, not constituting a law that interferes, and that we should focus on simplification, because this is a real need pointed out by all entrepreneurs. They expect not a spokesperson, but simple, clear rules of the game, not to change the VAT bill, and in the last 12 years 44 times this has happened, only clear and predictable rules. And that's what we care about. Therefore, the government has recently presented a plan of 100 changes for simplifying and facilitating businesses. Among them were among others. solutions for reducing certain information obligations, clarifying issues that raise interpretation doubts, changes to support the development of entrepreneurship, improving the efficiency of work, improving the investment process or reducing control burden. Secondly, there is reasonable doubt as to the duplication of the Ombudsman's duties with the obligations of the Ombudsman under the law. Here we have both the similarity of instruments and the similarity of activity, the object of action. The entrepreneur is a citizen and can make his reservations and comments to the Ombudsman. Thirdly, there is also another office to guarantee, on the one hand, the conditions of competitiveness, on the other hand, the settlement of concentrations. It is the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. Here again we see some overlap of competences and thus the creation of these overregulations. In this regard, once, in a not so distant past, the applicant was a spokesperson and was responsible for this in the Ministry of Economy. Finally, another super-regulatory element, which has also been reported here, is that when we appoint an ombudsman, we do not define in the draft law a group of entities, who is an entrepreneur. We know that we find this in many legal acts. However, the lack of such clear clarification may raise doubts as to the extent of the protection taken by the ombudsman. Finally, there is no such thing as a cost-free administration. The justification is that the appointment of an office will not cost anything. I think that every Member who has an internship longer than I am at the place where I am now - there are Members who have also served as ministers - knows that there is no administration that costs nothing. As regards the transfer of these costs, they will either be generated for the state budget - they will, in fact, be - or will be transferred to entrepreneurs. There is a declaration that the costs associated with the Ombudsman's activities will be covered by the Labour Fund, and let us remember that the Labour Fund has its tasks and objectives to fulfil, and therefore it will most likely involve an increase in the contribution. This calls into question whether the ombudsman will really fulfil the fundamental task of reducing the level of obstacles to economic activity. In view of the above reservations, which submitted to the Ministry of Development to consider the other members of the government before preparing the position of the government, we recommended that work on the bill be discontinued. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In the case analysed, none of the circumstances indicated in that provision are present. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u555	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, It's my honor to introduce you. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] Government draft retail tax law contained in print No 615. The bill submitted introduces a new public tribute to the Polish tax system. The proposed legislation has been subject to extensive public consultation. During the meetings held, also in the Sejm at the invitation of the Parliamentary Team for the Support of Entrepreneurship and Economic Patriotism, discussions were held with representatives of the business community on optimal solutions. The proposal submitted to the High House is the result of this consultation. The main objective of the project is to increase the tax revenue of the State budget for the financing of budgetary expenditure under the As a government, we also hope to reverse the negative trend, which is the constant decline in the number of small shops which are often the sole source of Polish families' livelihood. High Izbo! We propose the introduction of a new income tax directly charged to retailers with monthly revenues of a certain amount. According to the project, traders selling to consumers will be regarded as retailers. The taxation will be subject to the payment of disposals of goods, goods or parts of goods carried out both at the premises and outside the premises of the trader, e.g. at fairs or presentations. The project does not provide for taxation of online sales. The tax base will be the surplus of sales revenue of more than PLN 17 million in a given month. This means that every month there will be an equal amount free from which the seller will not pay the tax. As soon as this income is achieved, the surplus, calculated on a monthly basis separately, over this amount will be taxed. So that the obligation to submit a tax declaration will apply only to taxable persons who will earn revenue in a given month in excess of the amount free. The revenue shall be determined in principle on the basis of the volume of turnover checked by means of a register. Revenue will not include the tax due on goods and services. Sales to traders due to their economic activities will not be included in the tax base. The project provides for exemption from taxation of revenues from the paid disposal of energy materials such as natural gas, electricity, heat, water delivered to consumers via distribution networks, solid fuels, gas in fuel tanks and fuel oil. In addition, it is envisaged to exempt from taxation the sale of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices refunded or financed in whole or in part from the State budget. The project introduces two tax rates: first, the 0.8 rate for the tax base up to PLN 170 million, second, the 1.4 rate for the tax base over PLN 170 million. High Izbo! As I mentioned earlier, discussions on retail tax solutions in Poland lasted for many weeks. They also concerned the choice of a tax model, including the application of a linear rate or tax progression. The proposal currently submitted to the High House is a compromise between the original concept of several tiers of progress with higher taxation on Saturdays and Sundays and a low amount of free and linear tax. The mild scale proposed in the project is primarily justified by the redistributive objective. Large-income companies generally have greater payment opportunities and can more often use optimization practices. For these companies there are also so-called companies. economies of scale: the higher the sales, the lower its unit costs are. Undoubtedly, their ability to pay tax is greater. At the same time, a relatively high tax-free amount of PLN 17 million per month will serve as a safeguard against the smallest trading facilities with the lowest profitability. It is expected that the Act will enter into force on 1 August this year, so September will be the first month in which the revenue to the State budget for this tax will take place. The government estimates that gross revenue from retail tax will amount to around PLN 630 million in 2016, while net revenue, i.e. less the loss of income from income taxes of PIT and CIT, due to the cost nature of the tax estimated at about PLN 510 million. On the other hand, gross revenue should be estimated at about PLN 1.9 billion per year and net revenue - at PLN 1.5 billion per year. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, During the drafting of the bill, the question of compliance with EU law was also examined. It should be pointed out that sectoral taxes imposed on shopkeepers exist in Member States of the European Union, such as France or Spain. We are in constant contact with the representatives of the European Commission. We explain to them how the tax works and point to the compatibility of the proposed rules with European Union rules, including those relating to State aid. Dear Marshal, High Seym! By passing the bill to the High House for consideration, I assure you by the government, [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] that we are ready to participate in parliamentary work with full commitment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u579	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the government bill on retail tax. The bill introduces a new tax, which is not present in the Polish tax system: retail tax. This is a tax announced by the government a few months ago as such a miraculous addition to the state budget's revenue for the implementation of the 500+ programme. The original tax proposals raised huge controversy. They have raised many protests from both commercial organizations, shops and simply small sellers. A number of comments and reservations were made. It should be admitted that the changes made by the government side... Today, the project is significantly different from the original version. So it was like looking for the final version of this tax. The tax will be subject, as we read in the explanatory memorandum, to stationary sales, i.e. distance sales, i.e. via the Internet, will not be subject to this tax. This is an income tax on retail sales charged to retailers. The tax base is to amount to a surplus of retail revenues of more than PLN 17 million in a given month. Differences have been applied, i.e. progressive tax, rates of tax, depending on the amount achieved over that amount of revenue. This is 0.8% income surplus tax between PLN 17 million and PLN 170 million per month and 1,4% tax on excess sales revenue exceeding 170 million monthly. The revenue from retail sales will be the amounts received by the taxable person for the sale, including various types of advances, instalments or prepayments. Income will not include VAT due, but will include excise duty on goods subject to VAT. This revenue will be determined on the basis of the volume of turnover recorded on the register in a given month. Tax excluded, among others. natural gas, water, coal and some of its derivatives, as well as fuel oils, gas hydrocarbons used for fuel purposes, medicines, foodstuffs, recovered medical products and goods disposed of as part of the provision of catering services. In fact, after the protests of franchise networks, which were to be taxed like large companies and large-scale shops, the government withdrew from these ideas by increasing the tax-free amount. The government estimates that the current form of the law will only affect large, partly medium-sized enterprises. However, I do not think, High Izbo, that the burden of this tax should not be passed on in the form of an increase in the prices of goods to ordinary consumers. And thus the government implements its constant principle of strengthening with one hand the law, already today the rights of distribution regardless of the financial situation of the family, and the other hand of pulling the same money out of the pockets of its taxpayers, its citizens, from the pockets of Poles. I have serious doubts as to whether such a mixing can result in something good. We will not, ladies and gentlemen, support this project for this reason. It is thoughtless, unrealistic and even harmful to both sellers and customers. Thank you. [[Applause]]

As every year in May and June, the Special Schools in Łomża organized a cyclical environmental event called Earth Days. This time it was the fourth installment. Since 2012, the concept of the project has evolved several times, but the coordinators have always had two priorities: the promotion of nature conservation and the promotion of regional culture. The aim of the project is to bring students, parents and friends of the school of natural values and traditions of the region closer to the beauty of the Podlasie land and to the implementation of ecological behaviour. The permanent point in the Earth Days program is the collection of electro litter, natural workshops, day trips to the nearest places related to the environment, e.g. Lomżyńskie Landscape Park of the Narwi Valley, to the Museum of Nature in Drozdów, to the agrotourism complex Olszynk and Czarnocki Bud, natural lessons by experienced foresters from Nadrześnictwo Łomża, handicraft workshops and folk festival. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u651	Yes, after the delivery. [[Joy in the Chamber]] Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I was honoured to make a statement in the Seym of the Republic of Poland in connection with the 1050th anniversary of Poland's baptism. These events are well known and widely discussed. All Members also took part in the National Assembly in Poznań called for this occasion. In my speech I would like to draw attention to the less famous circumstances of Poland's baptism, which are connected with Western Pomerania. Although baptism is mainly associated with Gniezno, Wielkopolska, it was conditioned by the situation in the West Pomerania and the Oder Pomerania. Historians see the beginning of the path to Christianization of Poland at the start of the Polish-Vielec fights, which took place about the Western Pomerania. Many were Slavic tribes living between the lower Oder and the Elbe. The fight began around 963, and the theatre of war was Lubuskie Land and Western Pomerania. Failures in this war forced the House, Prince Polan, to seek out allies. An ally found in the person of the Czech prince Bolesław, who sealed the Polish-Czech alliance with his sister Dobrawa and Mieszek. Since she was a Christian, Mieszko was baptized in 966, and the following year his son was born, who received the name Bolesław, and later got the nickname of Krzywousty and became famous as the Polish ruler who made the Christianization of Pomerania. High Izbo! As inhabitants of the Polish Western Pomerania we remember this heritage, which involves the baptism of Poland. Pope John Paul reminded us of him in 1991. II, who in Koszalin at the airport, at the beginning of the fourth apostolic journey to Poland, said that standing on this land, faced a significant part of our history, at the very beginning of the history (...) at the extension of the baptism which Poland adopted in 966. The Pope reminded us of the role of Kołobrzeg, who together with Krakow, Wrocław and Poznań formed the Gniezno metropolis, which became the foundation of the organization not only of the Church, but also of the Piast State. At that time, it was a condition for one's own statehood and belonging to the European family to have a permanent and independent ecclesiastical organization. It was created 34 years after the baptism, in 1000 years, during the Gniezno convention. It is worth recalling that the Czech Republic, which had previously entered the orbit of Christianity from Poland, had its own ecclesiastical organization, archbishopship in Prague, only in the 14th century. The Germans had not been able to accept the independence of the Polish metropolis in Gniezno for a long time, and it was only in 1136 that the Pope's final recognition of it. These historical events at the beginning of the Polish statehood the Pope called the foundation on which we should build a common Europe. Today I would like to quote in the Seym of the Republic of Poland the very important words of John Paul II in Koszalin: "Let this reminder from the distant past of nations in the centre of Europe become today a new wish and announcement of the European future at the threshold of the new millennium. May the spirit of Emperor Otto and King Boleslaw bound by the memory of Saint Wojciech by mutual brotherhood and respect return to our history as the cornerstone of peace and cooperation. High Izbo! Let us not forget this papal wish, but also the memento. Let us appreciate the Western Pomerania, let us be aware of its geopolitical significance, let us oppose the attempts of historical manipulation in the execution of foreign propaganda in Poland. Let us take care of the Western Pomerania, let us not push it to the economic and social periphery, but let us do everything to make it flourish, it is the pride of Poland. Remembrance historical experiences and the common past, let us build the foundations of European cooperation based on mutual brotherhood and respect. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

Today's statement I would like to remind you of the forgotten strike in Namysłowskie Zakłady Elektrotechniky Motorizowej (ZEM). Until this year, none of the historical studies reported more widely about the strike that took place in 1976 at the ZEM, commonly referred to as "Powers." The memory of these events in a broader context was restored only this year by Dr. Mariusz Patelski. In 1975, the halls of the ZEM were in poor technical condition threatening construction disaster, and production stopped. In April 1976, on the so-called. 171 workers from 586 employed in total remained at the station. In addition, the situation was ignited by the price regulation announced in June 1976, which triggered a spontaneous outbreak of a strike in the mental institution. From 25 June to 10 p.m. 11 struck the entire first change of the ZEM, a total of 210 employees. The party officers tried to pacify the moods by organizing meetings in consultative groups, but the moods calmed down only after the Prime Minister spoke in the media. At the end of the strike, the party apparatus proceeded to clean up the ranks and determine the guilty. On the recommendation of KW PZPR, persons who refused to carry out certain activities were dismissed from the management positions of the PZPR, a list of employees who had taken the wrong attitude from 24 to 30 June 1976 was issued with an order to dismiss these workers. A total of 10 were dismissed, 12 were removed and moved to another job, one was terminated, 36 were reprimanded, and three were reprimanded. A total of 62 employees of the Zem Minds were punished, who took part in the June strike. The repressions of the communist apparatus illustrate the example of one of the displaced workers, the father of two young children who borrowed to renovate the house. After losing his job at the ZEM for many months because of the so-called. The wolf ticket couldn't find a job. Eventually, he became depressed and had to undergo psychiatric treatment. Here, in this Parliamentary Chamber, on the 40th anniversary of the strike in the Mental ZEM, I would like to pay tribute to the workers of this plant who stood up for dignity and workers' rights. Particular attention should be paid to the repressed, persecuted, vilified, and above all to their families. [[Applause]]

Each year, on June 14th, the World Blood Career Day established by the World Health Organization is celebrated on the anniversary of Karl Landsteiner, Austrian immunologist, Nobel laureate who discovered blood groups in 1901. At this point, I ask the ladies and gentlemen of the House to involve themselves and the people closest to you in promoting the idea of blood donation, all the more so as the beginning of the holiday period should be a reason for all of us to further mobilise. The annual summer months are those during which the amount of blood given significantly decreases. Regional blood donation and blood treatment centres increase promotional activities at this time and encourage special attention to this important problem. I would like to join these calls and encourage all of you to join this good initiative. At the same time, I call for all of us to supply ourselves with a blood type identification card, commonly referred to as a blood card. In the event of an accident, this small document allows you to determine the blood type of the injured paramedics and employees of the health care system in Poland. The blood-group identification card is a reliable document certifying the blood-type of the holder. Having a card reduces the waiting time for transfusions by about 30 minutes. It also avoids marking blood types during repeated visits to hospitals. At the same time, I would like to draw attention to the fact that in Poland there is still a systemic solution regarding the development of plasma surpluses. Currently only about 30% collected plasma is used for clinical purposes. The rest is a surplus that is used to produce blood products. Poland as one of the few already large European countries has not yet had its own plasma processing plant for blood products. On this day, I would like to draw the attention of the Prime Minister and the Minister of Health to take appropriate steps to organise in the country their own plasma processing plant for blood products. By taking this opportunity, I wish all blood donors and employees of regional blood donation and blood treatment centers to continue their determination and satisfaction in carrying out this wonderful gift of heart and every success. Let the feeling of unselfish help to those in need be a source of your satisfaction and joy. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u667	A statement by the Members on the 75th anniversary of the death of Ignacy Jan Paderewski. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 29 June we will celebrate the 75th anniversary of the death of Ignacy Jan Paderewski - composer, pianist, musician, statesman. Taking advantage of this opportunity, I would like to say a few words about the life of this Polish virtuoso and politics. Ignacy Jan Paderewski was born on 18 November 1860 in Kurylówka, Podol, in the Russian partition at that time. He had already demonstrated his musical abilities as a little boy. Between 1872 and 1878 he was educated at the Warsaw Music Institute, where he studied piano. After graduating, he became a teacher. He maintained himself by giving lessons of the game, impressive and performing his own songs, as well as playing at various celebrations. He constantly perfected his workshop. To this end, in 1881, after the death of Antonina Korsakovna, who had been married a year earlier, left for Berlin. Thanks to financial support from Helena Modrzejewska, a Polish actress fascinated by Paderewski's talent, the artist could continue his studies in Vienna and then take up the work of a piano teacher in Strasbourg. The composer's first success was his piano debut in 1887. A year later, his first big concert took place in Paris. The following years are performances in London, among others. before Queen Victoria herself, as well as the great tour of the United States. Paderewski decided to live in the United States while gaining great popularity across the ocean. He was called there, among others. Master and king of pianists. To this day, his only stage work of Manru remains the only opera composed by the Pole, which was staged at the American Metropolitan Opera. In 1899. Paderewski married Helena Maria Górska from Rosen's house. Young spouses bought a manor house in Kąśna Dolna near Tarnow, but settled in Switzerland, near Lausanne. The life of Ignacy Jan Paderewski is not only music, but also politics. He took up his diplomatic activity after the outbreak of World War I. He was then raising funds to help the victims of the war, and together with Henryk Sienkiewicz he founded in 1915. The Swiss General Committee for the Assistance of War Victims in Poland. He continued his work in the United States, where he continued to work in the United States. He gave a memorial about Poland to President Wilson. In 1917. Paderewski became a representative of the US National Committee of Poland. After returning to Poland, he took up the role of mediator between Dmowski and Piłsudski camps. As he said: "Stronage should be one: Poland, and this one I will serve to the death." In 1919. Paderewski became Prime Minister. As a representative delegate, he represented Poland at a peace conference in Paris concluded with the signing of the Treaty of Versailles. After Piłsudski's death in 1935. Paderewski co-created the so-called. Front Morges. After the outbreak of World War II, Paderewski became part of the Polish authorities in exile. He became President of the National Council of the Republic of Poland in London. He was active in Poland also in the USA. His name was placed by the Nazis on the list of enemies of the Third Reich in the so-called. The Black Book. Paderewski died of pneumonia on 29 June 1941. He was originally buried in the National Cemetery near Washington. However, on June 29, 1992, a coffin with its ashes was brought to Poland, where it rested in the crypt of the archicathedral of St. Jana in Warsaw. He was posthumously decorated with the Order of Virtuti Militari. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-22-sejm-21-2.u668	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On June 20, over a hundred countries in the world celebrated an annual festival called International Refugee Day. It was established on 4 December 2000 by the UN General Assembly. This choice was not accidental, as African Refugee Day was celebrated on June 20, 1969. Thus, the choice of June 20 was to be a commemoration and a reference to this event. Let me remind you that a refugee is considered to be a person forced to leave his homeland because of the threat of freedom, health and even life. Armed conflicts and persecutions for religious, political, social, or skin color are the main causes of such threats. The aim of the International Refugee Day was to highlight the courage, strength and perseverance of refugees around the world. The celebrations of that day would give rise to reflection on the difficult position of those leaving their country of origin often in fear, fear, and uncertainty about the fate of the future. Its aim was also to remind you of the fundamental protection of rights, as well as to help find a safe place in another country. It is worth noting, however, that this holiday also forces other reflections, namely to reflect on the location of those countries to which refugees are coming. The current influx of refugees into Europe is massive. This is the largest wave of this kind since World War II. For this reason, there are many doubts and concerns about the arrival of an increasing number of people from the Middle East to European countries. Statistics on refugees in Europe indicate, among other things, that around 900 000 entered Europe through the Mediterranean Sea in 2015. people, that is almost four times more than in 2014. These figures show the scale of the problem Europe must face, including Poland. Finally, the only question remains: Is Poland often called, among others. must the European Union accept such a solution for the reception of refugees? It is worth considering the consequences of receiving them into our homeland in the face of the International Day of Refugees. Are we aware of the gap between us, Poles and refugees coming from the Middle East? Do we see how much we share in cultural, religious, social matters? Are we aware of potential national conflicts, or are we aware of an increase in the potential epidemiological risk? The government of the Republic of Poland should take first into account the welfare of the Polish society and protect the safety of its citizens. Therefore, before making further decisions on these issues, let us consider the consequences for us and for future generations. Thank you very much.
